## Scope rulings

Each ruling is `Ruling: <decision> — <why> — <cost if wrong>`; the ids in parentheses are the requirement-map questions it settles. Task writers follow them; a task that finds a ruling impossible stops and reports instead of improvising. Plan 3 extends Plan 2's files and records; it never replaces a Plan 2 file, store or schema, and every Plan 2 ruling (R-01…R-39) and ledger ruling not amended here still binds.

**Sequencing and ownership (what Plans 4–7 keep, and the Plan 3 stub for each):**
- **R3-01 Plan 2 gate.** Ruling: Plan 3 runs on feat/p3-holds rebased onto feat/p2-engine after Plan 2's Task 14 fix round and Tasks 15–20 have merged and Plan 2's exit evidence is in its ledger; Task 1 first checks that every Plan 2 name this contract cites exists with the cited signature (a divergence stops the task for a controller ruling); Task 14's landed fix — the pathless `host-reject` PIN with origin `daemon` and the helpers `src/engine/treeDiff.ts`/`treeEdit.ts`, which landed with it and which Plan 3 reuses (Global Constraints, "Shared helpers") — is part of the baseline, and the binding Plan 2 text is the plan doc as overridden by the controller's `contract.md` (header) — the roadmap writes a plan against landed interfaces, and 10 of the 17 tasks (Task 12 is split into 12a and 12b) edit files that exist today only as Plan 2 contract (`loop.ts`, `status.ts`, `start.ts`, `restart.ts`, `recover.ts`, `daemon/run.ts`, the engine fixture, the SIM adapter, the generators, the seed runner) — cost if wrong: idle time; the controller may start Task 1, and then Task 3 (which depends on Task 1), before Tasks 15–20 merge, under R3-66's gate, because they touch only files that exist after Task 14 (`src/holds`, `src/board`, `src/ports/trace.ts`, `src/data/scope.ts`, `deps.ts`, `core.ts`, `apply.ts` and their tests) and Task 1's re-baseline test checks only the names Plan 2 Tasks 1–14 produce; the names of Plan 2 Tasks 15–20 are checked by Task 2's first test, and Task 2 (which edits `loop.ts`) waits for them. (data X-01, OQ-17; baseline Q20)
- **R3-02 `/cowork resolve`.** Ruling: Plan 3 implements `resolve(core, it, id, verb, o)` with every Table 4 effect, HLD-57 (a)–(e) and every §1 refusal code, exposes it as `Engine.resolve`, and adds the stopgap `node dist/daemon.js resolve --repo <p> --branch <b> --id <hold_id> --publish|--discard|--park|--restore`, which, like R-28's `resume`, runs only when no daemon runs: it takes `daemon.lock` exclusively for its whole run (R-26's dead-pid replacement included, refusing when a live daemon holds it) and runs `restart(core)` — intent resolution, own leftovers, `settleHoldOps` — before its fresh fetch, as Plan 2's Task 17 carry requires of every `restart()` and of a separate `daemon.js recover` (a human at a terminal is the human evidence); Plan 7 builds the `/cowork resolve` command over IPC, SEC-25 evidence, preview/confirm ids and `/cowork review --accept`; Plan 5 binds `cowork_resolve(id,"restore")` to `claudeRestoreRefusal` plus `restore`; Plan 6 publishes the `request` of `--handoff` (Plan 3 changes only the offer); `/cowork done` stays Plan 2's `Engine.done()` — Plan 2 line 161 ("`/cowork resolve`" in Plan 3) and line 165 ("the `/cowork` verbs" in Plan 7) both hold under this split, HLD-58 fixes the semantics and LIF owns the verb surface, and F-HLD-1 needs the verbs in SIM — cost if wrong: Plan 7 re-plumbs the entry behind IPC with the same signature. (core Q1; cs Q10; data OQ-14; baseline Q3)
- **R3-03 The edit check.** Ruling: Plan 4 implements `editCheck(A, R, TIP, cs)` (REG-36) and binds `EditCheckPort`; Plan 5 owns the edit-checkpoint trigger, the hook flow and the 150 ms bound (06-claude §3.8.4); Plan 3 owns HLD-67 steps 1, 3 and 4, the HLD-39/HLD-40 readiness of passing captures, the open-CS T0 drop and INV-HLD-EDITCHECK, behind `EditCheckPort` bound to `grammarlessEditCheck` (checks 1–3 pass, check 4's size clause applies) — the three ownership sentences (04-regions status line, 06-claude §3.8.4, HLD-67) are then all true, and INV-REG-EDITCHECK makes a weaker check change only timing, never pushed bytes — cost if wrong: Plan 4 rebinds the port; no production edit checkpoint fires before Plan 5. (cs Q1, CS-10; baseline Q1)
- **R3-04 T0 and K7.** Ruling: Plan 3 owns the 01-engine §3.12 step-4 slot — T0 on every OUT and after every CAS re-merge, a failing group becomes a PIN with guard `t0` and composition restarts at step 3 without it, `incomplete` never passes, and an edit-checkpoint capture of a still-open CS is dropped instead (no PIN, no pushed message; rev 2.5, X-06/X-07: the drop records `last_check{kind: 'stale'}` from `T0Verdict.findings[0]` in the memory-only store, D1) — plus HLD-50 item 3 (`t0Shrink`, attributed per R-25) and the K7 PIN; items 1–2 sit behind `T0RegionsPort` bound to `passT0Regions` until Plan 4 — Plan 2 line 1259 ("T0 Plan 3") and 04-regions line 188 (items 1–2 are REG's) — cost if wrong: no grammar-based K7 before Plan 4, as in Plan 2. (core Q17; cs Q4, CS-20; baseline Q2)
- **R3-05 Seat change-sets without seats.** Ruling: Plan 3 builds the change-set table with kinds `human | recovered | seat`, the `CsSignal` set of 06-claude §3.12.1, `Engine.signal()` and the ENG-13 out-of-cycle iteration, and tests seat CSs, multi-CS publication groups, edit checkpoints and K7 with scripted signals at unit and integration level; Plan 5 feeds the same `CsSignal` shape from hooks; the exit profile `holds` stays human-only — HLD-34/37/39/40/67 need a signal source and FakeHost is roadmap row 5; freezing a hook-side contract in the exit SIM would pre-empt Plan 5 — cost if wrong: a multi-CS group defect surfaces in Plan 5's SIM instead of Plan 3's. (cs Q3; baseline Q6)
- **R3-06 Offers.** Ruling: Plan 3 ships the HLD-09 table (`offerNext`), `HoldRegistry.offerEvent`, `declines`, the owner-only mirroring of offer changes and `--handoff`'s refusal rules (the COO-10 "allows a `ckind: dependency` request" predicate answers false); no Plan 3 code fires a promoter, an answer, a `request_status` or a `presume_absent`, so a Plan 3 record reaches `pending_leftover` only in tests — HLD-09's triggers are seat and request events (Plans 5–6) — cost if wrong: none (the table is pure). (core Q2; cs CS-40; baseline Q6)
- **R3-07 Claims and the sender-side PINs.** Ruling: the B1 slot of Algorithm P step 2 calls `PublishGatePort`, bound to `openGate` (never blocks; the `claim` PREDICATE `claimStillBlocks` answers true, fail closed, and no Plan 3 code creates a `claim` record); the K2, K3/K6 and SHADOW record shapes have constructors and dedupe rules (`pinCs`, `shadowRecord`) exercised by unit tests only; `SymbolPort` is bound to `noSymbols` (HLD-48/HLD-49 symbol branches off, the path branch on) — roadmap row 4 builds claims and B1; HLD-06 keeps claims out of every hold computation — cost if wrong: 05 I2, I3 and I16 move to Plan 4. (cs Q7, Q9, CS-33…CS-36; core D6a)
- **R3-08 Everything else deferred, with its stub.** Ruling: Plan 4 — claims, `gate()`, T0 items 1–2, the symbol delta, `claim-breach` creation (MASK already pins it, HLD-08); Plan 5 — hook attribution (`humanOnlyAttribution`: `observedEdit` and `hookAttributed` answer false), fences, leases and TXNs (`TickInput.skip` and the defer reasons `fence`, `read_lease`, `txn`, `conflicted_leased` stay empty), the 150 ms capture bound, the seat-hook codes `HOLD_PIN_CONTEXT` and `HOLD_NOT_SHARED` (Plan 3 only declares them, R3-65), delivery of `INCOMING_WAITING`, `CS_UNPUBLISHED_NUDGE`, `CS_GROUP_WAITING`, `CS_READY_CANCELLED` (Plan 3 writes these four to status and log), `unacked(seat)` (*superseded by X-13: the one source is Plan 5's `TurnItems.unacked`; Plan 3 declares no `Engine.unacked`*), the G1 deny over `Engine.heldRanges`; Plan 6 — `wake-scope` creation and the wake work set (the registry accepts and masks the guard; `--handoff` refuses it; X-41: Plan 6 creates the wake-scope PIN through Plan 3's `pinCs('wake-scope', …)` inside its `backstop` binding, Plan 5's `plan5WakeScope` creates none), request publication, the K6 timer, templates; Plan 7 — base moves, branch escape and `base-move` (HLD-56), the save view (HLD-65), the stop and uninstall verbs (Plan 3 exposes `durabilityReport`, `parkAllForStop`, `discardHolds`), `filter-failure` and `secret-scan` creation and release (the registry accepts both, `h1` omission included), `/cowork review --accept`, OS notifications (`NT_HELD_DECISION`, `NT_PARKED`), doctor, L-CODES/L-TEMPLATES and the EPOCH_BASE change that ends a `generated?:exclude` record (implemented, unreachable); the 05-holds §8 defaults Q2, Q8, Q11, Q13, Q14, Q15 and Q16 are carried as written, except that Q14's "test B3's rev 2.4 variant measures the growth" (loose-object growth of failing edit-checkpoint candidates against the W6 soak gate, ≤ 50 MB per busy peer-hour) is Plan 7's soak measurement, with Plan 5's hook-fed checkpoints, because a measurement against a soak gate needs the real hook cadence and the soak harness and Plan 3 has neither (Plan 3 pins only that a failing candidate leaves no ref to its blobs, Task 10), and data OQ-25 is the "Names" Global Constraint — the roadmap rows and Plan 2 lines 162–165 — cost if wrong: none; each stub is a port binding or an unused primitive with its own unit test. (core A6, K7, L1, M1, M2, Q24; cs Q16, CS-48, CS-49, §12; data OQ-25, D-20, X-08; baseline Q10)

**Registry and records:**
- **R3-09 One closed guard table.** Ruling: `GUARD_TABLE` in `record.ts` is 05-holds Table 3 in row order with its class, the owner column verbatim (`HLD/REG`, `HLD`, `HLD/ENG`, `DAT`, `DAT/HLD`, `DAT/SEC`, `SEC (local human, glossary §16)`, `DAT/COO`, `ENG`, `LIF`, `SEC (local human)`, `local human`), the `{REASON}` key, the whole-path flag and the human-release flag; `hold()` accepts every row with the 05 §2 argument grammar; a guard name outside the table read from a record is a DECISION guard this daemon never releases or transitions, so every resolve verb on such a record refuses with `HOLD_GUARDS_REMAIN` naming the guard; `Plan2Guard` and `PLAN2_GUARD_CLASS` stay exported as views of the table — HLD-05, glossary §9 `{name, class, owner}` — cost if wrong: a later plan's new guard needs a table row (one line). (core Q25, A8, C; cs CS-32)
- **R3-10 Guard-less births.** Ruling: `guard: null` is accepted exactly for records born OFF_DISK PARKED — the marker companion (§3.4 item 3), Plan 2's ring late-write PATCH (R-03 (iii), ENG-35), the collision take capture (Table 4) and the HLD-64 copy-restore of a foreign record that carries no guard (Task 11) — and refused for every other birth; an ON_DISK record can reach an empty guard set only by a release (RESTORE, `--publish`); HLD-05's "the single call without a guard" is flagged for a spec fix — the four call sites are each spec-mandated and none masks anything — cost if wrong: none (OFF_DISK records mask nothing). (core Q4; baseline Q15)
- **R3-11 The pathless `host-reject` PIN.** Ruling: the ledger form landed with Task 14 is kept: a board-only NONRETRYABLE creates a `host-reject` PIN with `paths: []`, `cs_ids: []`, origin `daemon` (`record.ts` admits a pathless record for that guard only, terminal records included); it masks nothing, S6 and `--park` skip it (nothing to write), its mirror is an ordinary op with `paths: []` whose PRO-21 `held/` requirement is vacuous (the fold requires a `held/<id>/` subtree only for a non-terminal, non-`local_only` record with at least one path), and `resume` ends it SUBSUMED with its guards unchanged (`host-reject` kept, never `releaseGuard`), so the terminal record and its terminal op still pass `checkRecord` and `parseOpLine`, which admit `paths: []` only with that guard (R3-39; the Plan 2 Task 14 ledger carry: "a transition that removes it terminates the record instead") — 05 F6 ("one PIN with guard `host-reject`"), the Task 14 ledger ruling and the landed code agree — cost if wrong: a stub-shaped op on the board for a zero-path record. (core Q3, Q19; baseline Q5)
- **R3-12 `local_only` is recomputed at every rev.** Ruling: the registry computes `local_only` itself at creation and at every transition from `localOnlyCauses(record, scopeOf(), isCompanion)` — a `filter-failure` or `secret-scan` guard, a path matching a U10 secret-like pattern, a path whose scope class is not LIVE, or a marker companion — reading the current iteration's scope through `RegistryDeps.scopeOf` (before the first iteration of a run — restart, lost-state rebuild, `settleMarkersParks` — it is the scope at `EPOCH_BASE` built from the start-time `AttrCache` and team config); `NewRecord.local_only` becomes optional and is ignored — HLD-03 ("recomputed at every rev"), DAT-31 — cost if wrong: one scope lookup per path per transition. (core A6; data D-06, D-19)
- **R3-13 Registry-local bookkeeping.** Ruling: the registry tree gains one canonical-JSON blob `local.json` next to `records/` and `blobs/` holding what only this PC needs and the record schema cannot carry: `companions` (marker companion id → parent id), `explicit` (ids released by an explicit `--publish`/`--restore`, so R1 ends them RESOLVED_PUBLISH), and `rejected` (hold id → the mirror key and reason of a `HOLD_MIRROR_REJECTED`); it is written by an ordinary registry commit through `registry.cas`, never mirrored and never on the board — the glossary §9 schema is exact (no extra record field), HLD-01 allows only one store, and these facts must survive restarts — cost if wrong: one blob per registry commit. (core F5, D5, E1; baseline Q15)
- **R3-14 One change, one id.** Ruling: `holdOutcome()` attaches the new guard (one rev) to the non-terminal record of the same kind and path set whose `H1'` the new `H1` equals or REBASE-converges to (cache keyed `(id, rev, A[p])`); a K4 (stage) call on a path set already held by another record transitions that record to CONFLICTED with the stages attached instead of creating a record; R-02's stage dedupe on `s2` stays; join keys for HLD-46/47/49 dedupe are Plans 4–5's extension of `NewRecord` — HLD-07, INV-HLD-SINGLE, 05 P6 — cost if wrong: a REBASE per candidate record on every `hold()` (cached). (core A9; cs Q12, CS-41; data D-12)
- **R3-15 Markers park order.** Ruling: (1) one registry commit creates the marker companion (`h0 = A[p]`, `h1 = M[p]` from the staged-copy read, OFF_DISK PARKED, no guard, `local_only`, `companionOf = r`); (2) `writeLocalGroup(M → A)`, all or none; (3) on `WRITTEN` one commit releases `markers` on r and moves it OFF_DISK (PARKED, or CONFLICTED kept); on any other writer result the companion ends GONE in one commit (its bytes are still on r's disk) and r is unchanged; at restart a PARKED companion whose parent still carries `markers` is settled by observation — rows still on disk → companion GONE, disk equal to `A` → step (3) completes — §3.4 item 3 cannot be one registry commit literally because step 3 is a disk write; INV-NOLOSS needs the bytes in a record before the disk changes; HLD-16 forbids a half-parked record — cost if wrong: an extra companion commit on a failed park. (core Q5, D5; data D-17)
- **R3-16 RESTORE labels.** Ruling: RESTORE uses 05-holds §3.4 item 4's `-L cowork:disk -L cowork:base -L cowork:held`; 03-data §3.9's `cowork:current`/`cowork:yours <id>` is flagged for alignment — HLD-17 binds RESTORE to §3.4 item 4 and both forms start rows with `cowork:` (so `hasRestoreMarkerRow` and DAT-46 are unaffected) — cost if wrong: a label string. A clean (rc 0) text RESTORE writes `canon(result, ref(p))`, not the raw merge result: DAT-45 step 1 canonicalises before `writeLocal`, 05 §3.4 item 4 writes the raw result, and DAT-01 allows route (c) only for bytes already in stored form, so a disk blob D that is not in canonical form (a Write dropped the BOM and the repair has not run yet) would otherwise put a non-canonical blob into the store; the rc > 0 marker result is written as merged (it never leaves this PC). (core Q6; data OQ-8)
- **R3-17 R1 after a release.** Ruling: R1 step 3's `MASK_r(S_disk)[p]` is the step-5 reverse merge for r alone, `mf(S_disk[p], H1'[p], H0'[p])`, whatever r's guard set is; a record with an empty guard set after RESTORE therefore stays ACTIVE (unmasked, publishing) until SUBSUMED, which `local.explicit` turns into RESOLVED_PUBLISH — R1 step 3's purpose ("the held change is no longer on disk") and HLD-29 — cost if wrong: none observable (the other reading ends the record GONE and loses RESOLVED_PUBLISH). (core Q8, E1)
- **R3-18 Binary and filtered paths.** Ruling: no `merge-file` runs on a binary (DAT-04) or filtered path: an ON_DISK ACTIVE PATCH on such a path masks its whole path to `A[p]` (step-2 treatment), its REBASE is `H1' = H1` when `A[p] == H0[p]` or `A[p] == H1[p]`, else a conflict, and the same whole-path rule applies whenever any of `A[p]`, `H0[p]`, `H1[p]` is ABSENT (no `merge-file` runs), and its RESTORE is the binary rule (human only, `HOLD_RESTORE_CONFLICT` unless `DISK_EQ(p, b)` or `DISK_EQ(p, A[p])`) — DAT-20, DAT-35, the revert detector's binary PIN, §1 "binary path: only whole-path PIN records" — cost if wrong: a binary record withholds its whole file (the safe direction). (data OQ-6, D-16, D-21)
- **R3-19 MASK and REBASE are asynchronous git operators.** Ruling: `maskEntries(repo, X, inp, cache, o)` returns `Promise<MaskResult>` and writes no registry state — the record ids whose step-5 merge returned rc ≠ 0 come back in `conflicted` and the caller transitions them; `ChangeSets.composeP` takes the asynchronous mask; REBASE outputs and step-5 intermediates are hashed by route (c) and written (`-w`) because the next `merge-file --object-id` reads them, which is allowed because MASK and REBASE run only in materializing or publishing iterations; `S_pub` is the `MaskResult` of the last materialized `S_disk` held in memory (`EngineCore.sPub`) and exposed to the oracle as a `TreeMap` of ids — 05 §3.4 ("Between materializations `S_pub` stays the MASK of the last materialized `S_disk`"), glossary §26.1 W6, HLD-15's CONFLICTED side effect — cost if wrong: objects for step-5 outputs on materializing iterations (already W6 occasions). (data OQ-4, OQ-5, OQ-19; core D3; cs CS-19)
- **R3-20 The HLD-19 assertion.** Ruling: `assertSPub` runs on every `S_pub` computation and `assertP` on every composition of P (not at detection ticks that reuse `S_pub`); ENG-41 (i)'s `masked(p)` keeps meaning "withheld whole by a record" (steps 2–4 or a step-5 conflict): `masked(p) = MaskResult.withheld.has(p) && maskReason(p) !== 'path_class'`, so Plan 2's ratified split in `heldPathInP` stays — letter `i` for a record-withheld path, `INV-MASK` for a QUARANTINED/SAVE_ONLY/UNMANAGED path (step-1 paths stay in `withheld`, which `testMask` and the oracles read) — and step-5 paths are checked by HLD-19's held-range and `revert`-line clauses in `assertP`; a failure raises `HOLD_MASK_INVALID` into status and log, drops the code part of this loop's publish (an `assertSPub` failure also of every later window until the next passing `assertSPub`; an `assertP` failure only of its own window, R3-57) and still sends the allowed board-only ops of that window (hold mirrors keep flowing), never falls back to `S_disk` — HLD-19 ("no code push this loop (board-only pushes continue)") is the specific rule for this assertion; ENG-28's "abort every publish" is read as every code publish and flagged; 05 F1's "no push" holds whenever no board op is pending — cost if wrong: a board-only push in a loop whose code publish was refused. (core Q7, Q26, Q29, D8; data D-05)
- **R3-21 LOCAL_ONLY reads ON_DISK records.** Ruling: `ScopeInputs.localOnlyPaths` (D5 row 9) and `ConflictCtx.localOnly(p)` (D8 step 0, HLD-55) are bound to the paths of non-terminal ON_DISK `local_only` records only — MASK step 2 and INV-HLD-OFFDISK: an OFF_DISK `local_only` record (a marker companion, a collision take capture) masks nothing — cost if wrong: none (an OFF_DISK record's path is not on disk in its held form). (data OQ-7)
- **R3-22 K4 `displaced_by` and `peer_authors`.** Ruling: a K4 record's `displaced_by` is set, at its ON_DISK → OFF_DISK transition after the verified TIP write, to the op id `<peer>/<epoch>/<seq>` from the `Cowork-Writer` trailer of the newest live commit in `(LA_C, TIP]` that changed p, and `peer_authors` to that writer's peer id; both are set by `transition()`, the K4 dedupe key is unchanged — Table 2 row 1, glossary §9, `HOLD_CONFLICTED {author}` — cost if wrong: a display field. (core Q20, A3; baseline Q18)
- **R3-23 No local write over a kept push intent.** Ruling: PARK, S6, the markers park, RESTORE, the collision take, the disk repair and every resolve write report `POSTPONED` (and retry at a later loop, or refuse a resolve with `HOLD_DISK_CHANGED`'s next command "repeat the command") while `view.intent.kind === 'push'` or `Writer.strandedPaths()` is non-empty, and no capture is journaled and no candidate settled while `Writer.strandedPaths()` is non-empty (Plan 2's P2-STRANDED-SKIP carried: closing captures run only when `captureDue(core)` holds, whatever else made the iteration materialize) — the reasoning of P2-PUSHINTENT-FIRST and P2-NARROW: a `local` intent journaled over a kept push intent loses the record of a push that may have landed — cost if wrong: one loop's delay. (baseline Q19)

**Mirroring:**
- **R3-24 The `hold` op.** Ruling: the op carries exactly glossary §7's fields; `guards[]` are the record's `{name, class, owner}` objects verbatim (so an older reader can apply HLD-05 to an unknown name); a `local_only` stub keeps `paths[]` (names, not content) and carries no oid and no `held/` subtree; `declines`, `displaced_by`, `blobs`, `age_anchor` and `owner` are not in the op; `src/board/ops.ts` declares the op's unions itself (board imports nothing from holds) and a unit test pins them equal to `record.ts`'s — glossary §7, PRO-21 — cost if wrong: none. (core Q9, Q10, F1)
- **R3-25 When a record emits an op.** Ruling: a registry transition emits one `hold` op when it creates the record or changes its residence, state, offer, guard set or held side (`h1`/`s2`, which includes HLD-10's PIN H1 refresh at member close and the PARK refresh HLD-61 waits on); a change of `age_anchor`, `declines` or `displaced_by` alone emits none; an op whose draft has no seq yet is replaced by the newer draft for the same record (one pending op per record); an op with `prev_rev = null` is record-creating for PRO-06 and is withheld at the 5,000-record cap (`BOARD_FULL`) while ending and updating ops keep flowing; an op line over 16 KiB is a deterministic local rejection (`HOLD_MIRROR_REJECTED`, reason `validator`, never pushed) — HLD-10 ("never one per loop"), HLD-61 (the refresh must become durable), PRO-06, PRO-09, 02 §3.1 — cost if wrong: one extra op on a held-side change. (core Q11, Q28, F2, F9)
- **R3-26 Durability without a mirrored-rev field.** Ruling: `isDurable(r)` holds when the fold of the fetched board tip has `holds[r.id]` equal to r's op projection (every field but `rev`/`prev_rev`) and the tip's `held/<r.id>/` entries equal `heldBlobs(r)`; `local_only` records are never durable; a record with `paths: []` (the pathless `host-reject` PIN, R3-11) is durable when its folded entry equals the projection (no `held/` subtree is needed, so a null tip `held/` counts as `[]`); the engine re-mirrors (at start and at every publish preparation) every record whose mirror key differs from both the folded entry and its pending op, unless `local.rejected` holds that key — except that a terminal record gets an op only while the fold holds a non-terminal entry for its id or the record has a pending or allocated op (a terminal record with neither — one released before its creation op was drafted, R3-29, or one PRO-27 compaction dropped from the board — never gets an op, so an idle peer re-mirrors nothing and `record_count` never grows from re-created terminal records) — a revision that changes only `age_anchor` therefore never makes a record non-durable; the engine reads the tip's whole `held/` tree once per fetched board tip (`ls-tree -r`, cached by tip oid) and slices it per record — HLD-62 (a fold lookup, never `merge-base`), HLD-64 (re-mirror non-durable records), 05 §2 (age flushes are registry commits without an op) — cost if wrong: one `ls-tree -r` per new board tip. (core Q12, F6, F7)
- **R3-27 Own `hold` op rejections.** Ruling: every fold rejection for `prev_rev` ≠ folded rev is a race, never terminal: it re-drafts the op from the folded rev (at most one re-draft per record per folded rejection — each rejection is itself one landed board commit, so re-drafts are bounded by landed commits and cannot loop within a window); no log line is written (Plan 2's `Log.write` takes a registry code and none exists for a race; the re-drafted op shows in the `mirror` trace event); every other reason (`validator`, `held_subtree`, `local_only_stub`, and `generic`/`type` by R3-70) records `local.rejected[id] = {key, reason}`, raises `HOLD_MIRROR_REJECTED {hold_id, reason}` once (PRO-38), keeps the bytes and the record ON_DISK (not durable, never auto-parked), and drafts nothing more until the record's mirror key changes; a restart does not re-issue a rejected key — PRO-24 bullets 2–3, HLD-10 (only non-race rejections are terminal), HLD-62, 05 F5; a second race is possible (two consecutive ambiguous pushes of one record, a re-anchor), and a record left non-durable by it could never be stopped cleanly or auto-parked — cost if wrong: one extra board op per race. (core F5)
- **R3-28 The `--discard` record on the board.** Ruling: Table 4's "logged as an `event` and mirrored" is the mirrored `hold` op with state RESOLVED_DISCARD (a stub for `local_only`), a daemon log line and a `discard` trace event naming the held-side blobs; no new `event` kind — glossary §7 has no discard event kind; INV-NOLOSS reads the folded RESOLVED_DISCARD — cost if wrong: Plan 7 adds an event kind. (core Q15)
- **R3-29 INCOMING and transient creation ops.** Ruling: an INCOMING record's creation `hold` op is the board health stub of HLD-54 (no separate `event{kind: health}`); not-applicable INCOMING records are created at once but their creation op is drafted only once the record is 60 s old (`creationDue`); unreadable/unstable PINs and closure records are created at 60 s; a record released before its creation op was drafted emits nothing, neither then nor at any later start, restart or publish re-mirror (R3-26's terminal-record rule) — HLD-10, HLD-54 ("removed in rev 2: the separate health event"), INV-HLD-BUDGET — cost if wrong: none. (core Q22, F2)

**Receive side:**
- **R3-30 The 60 s clocks.** Ruling: transient and deferral ages are Δmono anchors in `TransientClocks` (in memory, restarted at daemon start, so a deferral older than 60 s gets its record ≤ 60 s after a restart); `meta.pending` keeps its Plan 2 rows and semantics while an INCOMING record covers a path (R-05); `status.json.pending.incoming` counts each path once (record or a deferral younger than 60 s) — ENG-05 (no timestamps in meta), HLD-32 "local monotonic", R-05 — cost if wrong: a restart delays a record by up to 60 s. (core Q16; data OQ-13; baseline Q17)
- **R3-31 One INCOMING record per closure.** Ruling: a closure deferred ≥ 60 s becomes one INCOMING record over all its applicable paths (H0 = A, H1 = TIP blobs, refreshed by a new rev when TIP moves), its guard chosen by the closure's reason — `eol_form` → `eol-form`, `local_only_conflict` → `local-only-conflict`, every other apply-side reason → `lock`; a closure is one deferred write group, never one `WriteGroupsResult.deferred` entry: `applyStep` regroups Plan 2's per-(group, reason) entries into one `ApplyReport.closures` entry per group whose paths are the union of that group's entries and whose reason is the group's non-`closure` reason, a group with several such reasons taking the first of `eol_form` > `local_only_conflict` > every other reason (→ `lock`), and the `apply` trace's `groups[].reason` carries the same group-level reason (Plan 2's `writeGroups` splits a group `[a, b, c]` with `b` locked into `{[b], locked}` and `{[a, c], closure}`, which would otherwise give two clocks and a guard-less record); each not-applicable path gets its own record at once (`unmaterializable`, `collision`, or `quarantine-review` with `local_only` by the LIVE-only rule); a record ends SUBSUMED when its paths apply or TIP no longer changes them — HLD-54, INV-GROUP ("a deferral produces one INCOMING record covering the whole group") — cost if wrong: none. (core Q27, K1, K2; data D-22)
- **R3-32 The incoming form check.** Ruling: `formCheckIncoming` keeps 03-data §3.10's chain signature but decides per path on `(A, TIP)`: a text, unfiltered path present in A and TIP violates iff `form(TIP[p])` is neither `form(A[p])` nor the form of a base-move commit's second parent on the chain that changed p; its write group defers at once (`eol_form`) and the INCOMING `eol-form` record comes at 60 s — the per-commit reading would re-flag the commit that restores the form (I-DAT-13 must release), and HLD-54 is the specific timing rule over DAT-11 — cost if wrong: a commit that violates and is fixed inside one `LA_C..TIP` span is applied (its net form conforms). (core Q23, K4; data OQ-2, OQ-3)
- **R3-33 Creation targets that already match.** Ruling: an incoming creation whose target exists locally and satisfies `DISK_EQ(p, TIP[p])` is applicable with no write (`A[p] := TIP[p]`, cause `disk_equal`), so the collision take and a keep-local file that became equal end without a second collision record — DAT-29 (keep-local ends on `DISK_EQ(p, TIP[p])`), ENG-36 `disk_equal`, Table 3 (take-team ends once `A[p]` equals TIP's blob) — cost if wrong: none. (data OQ-9, D-08, D-09)

**03-data record creators:**
- **R3-34 Disk repair.** Ruling: DAT-08's "2 snapshots" means two consecutive detection ticks with p's stat and detection id unchanged after the materialization that showed `S_raw[p] ≠ S_disk[p]`; the repair runs only in an iteration that materializes p, writes `S_disk[p]` through `writeLocal(p, S_raw[p], S_disk[p])`, then sets the private-index entry of p to `S_disk[p]` (the new `Snapshotter.setEntry`, because `writeLocal` leaves the private index alone) and calls `Snapshotter.markClean`; never under FREEZE, never on a path held under `eol-bom` or canon-refused; `EOL_REPAIRED {path, bom, eol}` goes to the daemon log only, deduplicated per (path, episode) for Plan 5's seat delivery — W6, the Task 10 ledger ruling (writeLocal leaves the private index alone), R-27 — cost if wrong: a repair one tick later. (data OQ-1, OQ-24, D-01)
- **R3-35 `portability` and `generated?` records are Plan 3's.** Ruling: Plan 3 creates the DAT-33 `portability` record (a `local_only` ON_DISK PATCH, whole-path, content-free stub, `PATH_NOT_PORTABLE`) for every NOT_ADMITTED `portability` path that is `stageable()` (a path no record can carry is R3-53's) and the DAT-39 `generated?` record (an ON_DISK PATCH, `GENERATED_SUSPECT`), with their releases and Table 4 special cases; a `portability` record's path counts as a member path of the open human CS for grouping (`portabilityStep` calls `cs.observe([p])`, the one exception to the Task 11 ledger rule "observe() only for LIVE paths"; R and A_cap both lack p, so the capture carries no change for it, and `Composition.withheldWhole` counts a group path covered by a MASK step 2–4 record even when R[p] == A[p]), so HLD-42 pins the rest of that CS (DAT-32: "never publishes the deletion alone"); the churn verdict uses the new `AttributionPort.hookAttributed`, false for every human delta, and F-DAT-04's autosave typing stays below the 20 % rows rule — roadmap row 3 covers chapter 03, both guards are Table 3 rows, HLD-42 and TC-HLD-10 depend on them, R-06 left the record out of Plan 2 — cost if wrong: two creators moved to Plan 7. (core Q13, Q14; data OQ-15, D-10, D-20; baseline Q12)

**Detectors:**
- **R3-36 Provenance and observed writes.** Ruling: `ProvenanceJournal.advance(old, new)` runs after every state CAS that moves `laC` — apply, push ok, the restart decision, the lost-state and re-anchor rebuilds — at the call sites R3-59 names (for an apply after its `apply.state` crashpoint); it appends one entry per live commit including this peer's own (so "writer ≠ this peer" stays exact after a teammate re-adds a line) and prunes entries older than 14 days at append; the journal is a rebuildable cache (glossary §6) whose rebuild (missing, corrupt, re-anchor) covers only `(rebuildFrom, laC]`, `rebuildFrom` being the `laC` of the newest state-ref reflog entry whose local timestamp is older than now − 14 days (this PC's clock against itself, INV-CLOCK), else the join point (R3-59: the live code of the oldest reflog entry's `laB`) — HLD-25's "≤ 14 days … back to the newest commit applied before the retention cut, or the join point", so a rebuild never re-protects a teammate line older than the retention for 14 more days; the load points, the advance sites and the crash behaviour (a kill at `apply.state` leaves the journal one commit behind, which `load` advances) are R3-59's. Every Plan 3 `writeLocal`/`writeLocalGroup` write, whatever its origin (`park:`, `markers_park:`, S6 and the stop park, `restore:`, `take:`, `discard:`, `repair:`), is recorded as attributed (01-engine §3.11, ENG-37 "MUST record provenance with the caller's origin"; the Plan 2 Task 10 ledger handed the recording to Plan 3): the operator calls `ObservedWrites.record(p, written)` after `WRITTEN`, and the detectors (`breakerTick` through `peek`, `revertStep` through `consume`) treat a delta landing exactly on it as observed (HLD-26 (b)); the park family (`park`, `markersPark`, S6, `parkAllForStop`) and `--discard`, which publish nothing new, also set the private-index entry of p to the written blob with `Snapshotter.setEntry` after `WRITTEN` (as R3-34 does for the repair) and commit R3-60's snap-only state transition, so the written paths are no unobserved delta in this run or after the restart that follows a stop (whose reseed reads `state:snap`) and open no human CS; a crash that loses an `ObservedWrites` entry of a restore or take can only create a `revert` record (the safe direction) — HLD-25 ("appended when LA_C advances"), ENG-36/ENG-37, the Task 10 ledger ruling — cost if wrong: one extra `revert` record after a crash during a restore. (cs Q13, CS-22, CS-23; data D-14)
- **R3-37 The detector is the spec's, including on human edits.** Ruling: the revert detector lands after MASK step 5 (Task 2), so a `revert` PATCH masks only its hunks and "untainted edits on the same path publish normally"; every human delta is unobserved (HLD-26), so a human edit of a line a teammate added within 14 days becomes a `revert` record that only SUBSUMED, GONE or `/cowork resolve` ends — kept as specified (05 U3: "human save … yes"); Plan 2's `edits` and `faults` profiles keep running and may now end with `revert` records, and INV-CONVERGE stays valid because every ON_DISK record path is in `not_converged` — HLD-26…HLD-29, INV-HLD-NOREVERT — cost if wrong: none for correctness; the UX cost is the spec's. (cs Q14, Q15, CS-24; data OQ-18)
- **R3-38 MASS entry, storage and resume.** Ruling: entering `FREEZE(MASS)` keeps the tree of the last materialized `S_disk` before the tripping tick as `journal/keep/snap_pre_freeze` (every object of a materialized snapshot exists, so keeping it reachable is safe), posts one `event{kind: freeze, reason_code: MASS}` and raises `FROZEN`; `resume` removes `MASS` from `freeze_reasons` and leaves the keep entry, whose presence while `MASS` is unset is the "post-resume batch pending" flag; the first materializing iteration after it runs the revert detector over `diff(snap_pre_freeze, S_disk)` as unobserved deltas (tainted → `revert` records attached by HLD-07), lets the untainted remainder form one human CS that publishes under S1, drops the keep entry in the same state update and logs the residue ids; LIF §3.6's REBUILD for MASS is not applied — HLD-30, HLD-31 and 05 I7 ("then the formatting publishes and the revert stays held") are specific; LIF §2.1's two-entry pre-freeze form exists because an EPOCH freeze's snapshot ids were never written, which does not hold for a materialized `S_disk`; no `meta` field is added — cost if wrong: Plan 7 reads `snap_pre_freeze` instead of `pre_freeze`/`pre_freeze_base` for a MASS episode (flagged to LIF; settled by X-81: Plan 7 Task 7 reads `journal/keep/snap_pre_freeze`, and the LIF spec edit is in Plan 7's batch). (cs Q11, CS-29, CS-31; baseline Q4, Q9)
- **R3-39 Resume releases `host-reject`.** Ruling: `resume()` (behind R-28's `daemon.js resume` and `Engine.resumeAfterNonretryable`, which now delegate to it) clears `meta.nonretryable` and the rejection streak and releases the `host-reject` guard of the record `meta.nonretryable.hold_id` names, so the PIN's group publishes by HLD-52 and a new third rejection re-adds the guard by HLD-07; when that record is the pathless PIN (R3-11), resume instead ends it SUBSUMED with its guards unchanged (no `releaseGuard`; its terminal op carries `host-reject`) and `ResumeReport.hostRejectReleased` still names it — Table 3 `host-reject` ("human `/cowork resume` … a new NONRETRYABLE classification of it adds the guard again"), ENG-45 — cost if wrong: none (R-28 left the PIN in place pending this). (baseline Q4)

**Change-sets:**
- **R3-40 The change-set table in the state ref.** Ruling: the open-CS table is the canonical-JSON blob `journal/keep/cs/table.json` (a keep tree holding one blob, the `keep/fold/state.json` pattern, so `state.ts`'s keep grammar is unchanged): per CS its id, kind, seat and agent, origin, member paths, state, capture counter, publish count and `checks`, plus each seat's `turn_seq` and the peer-wide capture order of every ready pair; it is written only in state transitions (captures, closes, publishes; ENG-04), and `cs/<enc(b)>.json` is the cache written at every membership change and read at restart only when it names the current state commit; a missing or stale table makes every unpublished delta outside a ready pair one `recovered` CS; `ReadyPair.order` comes from the table (from `n` for a human pair when no table exists) and `journal/ready/` accepts seat cs_ids; when no table exists yet, `ChangeSets` builds the first one with `orders[cs_id] = n` for every journaled pair and `next_order = max(max n over journaled pairs, meta.cs_human_next − 1) + 1` and writes it in the first capturing state update that writes a table (a `captureStep` capture), so a group capture always sorts after an older Plan 2-style pair; while no table is journaled a capture keeps Plan 2's `order = n` and forms no multi-CS group (R3-68), and the restore semantics of the journaled table and the cache are R3-69's; `ChangeSets.capture` keeps Plan 2's call shape (`pins` optional, default `[]`; the result keeps `pair` = the first closing CS's pair beside the new `pairs`), so Plan 2's `start.ts`, `loop.ts` and test call sites compile unchanged, and Plan 2's loop never writes a table before Task 12a routes its captures through `captureStep` (Task 12b does `start.ts`'s `captureLocalWork`; Task 9's push-ok update writes the table keep entry only once a table is journaled), so `StateStore.commit`'s table rule never refuses their pairs — HLD-36 ("journaled in the state ref (truth), with `cs/<enc(b)>.json` as a cache"), glossary §4 `keep/<name>`, R-01's re-forming rule as the recovered fallback — cost if wrong: one more keep tree per transition; flagged for 01-engine §3.2 like R-14's fields. (cs Q2, CS-05; baseline Q8)
- **R3-41 Candidates.** Ruling: an edit-checkpoint candidate is built in the ENG-13 out-of-cycle iteration from a materialized `S_disk` over the CS's publication group, checked through `EditCheckPort`, and journaled as the group's ready pairs only on a pass (a fail leaves the state ref unchanged and creates no record, op, event or pushed message; it writes `ChangeSets.lastCheck` (D1, memory only, glossary §29.8; amended for rev 2.5 by X-06)); candidates are ordered by the 06-claude per-CS `cand_seq`; an apply that changes `A[p]` while a passing candidate waits rebases it like any ready pair (HLD-41) — 05 §3.4 and 01-engine §3.8 (the candidate is materialized), 04-regions §3.20 ("the capture is then rebased"); 06-claude's hook-time form is Plan 5's to reconcile (flagged; settled by X-36: this ruling stands — A, R and TIP of the candidate are objects — and Plan 5's spec edit SE5-03 aligns 06 CCI-56) — cost if wrong: Plan 5 swaps the candidate's content source behind the same `Candidate` shape. (cs Q5, Q6; baseline Q16)
- **R3-42 A CS with a SHADOW.** Ruling: while a CS has a non-terminal SHADOW, none of its mid-turn captures is ready (HLD-48's test cannot run without the symbol delta, so "would hold" is assumed); at the close HLD-48 decides with the T0 branch only — HLD-67's "while it would hold, that capture is not ready", 06-claude §3.8.4 skip 2 — cost if wrong: none before Plan 4 (no SHADOW exists). (cs Q7, CS-36)
- **R3-43 Trailers.** Ruling: `Cowork-CS` carries `seq` = the 1-based count of that CS's publishes (each checkpoint, then the close), `state=checkpoint` for a passing capture of an open CS and `state=closed` for the closing publish, `origin=human|prompt|wake`, `checks` = the result of the latest completed `checks.checkpoint` call of the CS before the capture (a `checks_run {ok}` signal: `pass` or `fail`), `skipped` when none ran (HLD-37; 06-claude §3.12.1 emits only green checkpoints, so the `checks_run` signal, which Plan 5 feeds, is a 05/06 gap recorded below); `Cowork-Seat` lists each contributing seat or `human`; Plan 2's ratified `seq=0` is replaced (nothing has been released) — HLD-37, 05 I12 — cost if wrong: none (membership never reads `seq`). (cs Q8, CS-06; baseline Q7)
- **R3-44 Publication groups replace P2-PIN.** Ruling: `ChangeSets.groups()` is HLD-38's closure over this peer's open and ready CSs plus every held PIN (non-terminal ON_DISK PIN with a non-empty guard set, `wake-scope` included) sharing a path; a group holding a PIN is not ready and publishes none of its paths; at the close of a CS whose group holds a PIN the CS joins that PIN as a bundle (paths ∪, `cs_ids` ∪, `h1` refreshed from R, one rev, one op) instead of journaling a ready pair, and HLD-49's "guard `dependency:<pin id>` on the same record" is not added because it would name the record itself (the bundle waits for the PIN's own guards; flagged); when the last guard releases and T0 passes the bundle publishes in one commit (HLD-52); HLD-42's dependency PIN is a separate record over the group's LIVE paths minus `local_only` record paths, never joined to a `local_only` PIN, and releases when its target is terminal, no longer withholds a group path, or carries `generated?:exclude`; when a `withheldWhole` entry leaves no LIVE path outside `local_only` record paths (a CS whose only path is a `portability` record's), no dependency PIN is created — the group has nothing to publish and stays unready until its withholding record ends — 01-engine §3.12 step 1, HLD-38, HLD-42, HLD-49, HLD-52; the ledger's P2-PIN cost note anticipated this — cost if wrong: a self-referencing guard that could never release. (cs CS-13, CS-15, CS-37, CS-38; baseline Q11)

**Escalation, status and lifecycle primitives:**
- **R3-45 S6, stop and drain.** Ruling: S6 parks only a record that `isDurable` reports durable and whose paths lie in no open CS, outside FREEZE and with no push intent kept; a PARK that must refresh the held side first commits that rev and parks only once it is durable (a later loop); the +10 min `HOLD_REVERT_HELD` health event is posted once per record, deduplicated against the fold and the outbox (INV-ONE-EVENT); `Engine.drain` flushes age anchors and never parks; Plan 7's stop calls `durabilityReport` and `parkAllForStop(leaveOpId)` after allocating the `leave` op's seq, so `displaced_by` names that op — HLD-60, HLD-61, HLD-63 (a daemon exiting on lease loss or `IDLE_NO_SESSION` must not park), Table 2 — cost if wrong: none. (core Q21, L1, L2, M1)
- **R3-46 `status.json` from records.** Ruling: `holds_active` = non-terminal non-INCOMING records, `holds_local_only` = those with `local_only`, `incoming` = INCOMING records plus closures deferred less than 60 s (each path once), `not_converged` = Plan 2's entries, unchanged (P2-CARRY carried paths, the `push_unresolved` cause of a kept push intent older than 60 s, phantom paths, quarantine review items, round-trip exclusions, the pause/FREEZE `blocked_publish` paths), extended by every path of every ON_DISK record (every guard class), every deferred or not-applicable path, every path of a ready group kept back by FREEZE or a publish pause (`blocked_publish`), and every R3-53 blocked path plus every path of a group its gate keeps back (`blocked_publish`), OFF_DISK records excluded; `cs_open`/`cs_ready` from the CS table; `HOLD_DIVERGED_REGIONS {n, age}` is a message of the degraded rung (R3-61: n > 0 means an ON_DISK record, whose paths make the state degraded); `CS_NO_CHECKS_CONFIGURED` in the ok state after `lastEvent` when `checks.checkpoint` is empty (read leniently) — HLD-24, ENG-57, INV-CONVERGE (Plan 2's oracle reads `not_converged`), INV-NOSILENT — cost if wrong: none. (core I3; cs CS-12, CS-30, CS-45; data T-06, T-10)

**SIM, oracles and CI:**
- **R3-47 The exit profile `holds`.** Ruling: 3 peers (odd peers `core.autocrlf=true`, peer 2 with `platform: 'win32'` on every OS), 60 generated steps, human edits only, each carrying a fresh marker and tagged informed or stale; ordinary edits touch the editing peer's own lines or pre-genesis lines; deliberate `edit_peer_line`, `stale_save` and `rewind` ops create `revert` records and a later `resolve` op settles most of them; one `formatter` op over more than max(10, 2 %) files followed by a scripted `resume`; FaultFs share-read and share-none locks held ≥ 60 virtual s; locally ignored files colliding with incoming creations; non-portable names; a test-only non-conforming writer; `age_24h` jumps; `gc_prune`; kill at crashpoints p = 0.02 per step; then quiescence; no seat signals (R3-05) — HLD-26 makes every human delta unobserved, so an unconstrained generator would end every seed full of DECISION records and leave INV-GROUP's deferral clause and most INV-NOLOSS locations vacuous — cost if wrong: the profile misses a shape that Plan 5's seat profile then covers. (cs Q14; data OQ-12, OQ-23, OQ-26, X-02; baseline Q14)
- **R3-48 INV-NOLOSS, operationally.** Ruling: every marker written to a synced path must be in at least one of the six glossary locations (several at once is allowed, TST-08's "at least one" plus the two-stores rule), and no record id may hold bytes in two stores; the author's disk counts only while the marker's path belongs to an open CS of the author or to a captured but unpublished ready pair (`inspect().csTable` and `ready`, 11-testing §3.4: "an undisplaced edit still on its author's disk (a seat's open CS or human CS …)"), so once that CS's publish lands the marker must be at `live`, `held`, `registry` or `local_only_disk`; a marker written to a path that is not synced at the write (temp, secret-like without a record, locally excluded, NOT_ADMITTED without a record) is recorded exempt by the reference model and is no obligation (glossary INV-NOLOSS: "every byte … in a synced path"); a non-terminal, non-`local_only` record counts in its owner's local registry until the owner's first landed push after the record's creation rev, and indefinitely while the owner is offline or the record is `HOLD_MIRROR_REJECTED`, after which its held side must be a `held/` entry on the board; a `local_only` record counts on its owner's disk, a marker companion by its registry `h1`; the reference model retires a marker only on an informed edit by a peer whose A or disk held it, and INV-NOLOSS also fails when a marker in the model's `retired()` set (retired, with no noted re-add since) appears in `live_code(board tip)` — the resurrection clause (R3-64); "the saved branch" and "a seat's open CS" are vacuous until Plans 7 and 5 — TST-08, the 05 §5 harness extension, INV-HLD-DURABLE ("after one governed push") — cost if wrong: a weaker oracle that Plan 7's nightly tightens. (data OQ-10, OQ-11, OQ-12, T-01)
- **R3-49 Which oracles run.** Ruling: every SIM run asserts Plan 2's six exit oracles, the three exit oracles, O-READY, O-AND, O-OFFDISK, O-LIVE-RECORD, O-ATOMIC-PUB (human CSs; exceptions (a)–(c)), O-NOMARKER's held side, the held-bytes INV-SECRET slice (no restore-marker row and no `local_only` held byte in any remote object) and INV-NOSILENT's `status.json` half; O-EDITCHECK, O-WAKETURN, INV-FENCE and INV-HLD-CLAIMFREE wait for Plans 4–6 — TST-07 ("every SIM run MUST assert the oracles of §3.4") — cost if wrong: SIM time, kept down by recomputing INV-MASK only on loops that materialized or changed A or the registry, and INV-NOLOSS incrementally per op with a full scan at quiescence. (data OQ-20, OQ-21, OQ-22, T-04, T-05; baseline Q13)
- **R3-50 Mutants.** Ruling: `MutantId` gains M19 (step 5 from the unrebased pair), M25 (quarantined paths not reset), M28 (step 5 replaces the held rows by their original row numbers instead of the reverse merge — a positional per-hunk withdrawal), M33 (no mass-change breaker), M38 (apply writes the available paths of a group with a locked path) and M39 (no apply coalescing), each at exactly one site (R-29); M39 is killed through INV-GROUP's added clause "≤ 1 apply per 10 s per worktree" (glossary §20); INV-NOLOSS is paired in TC-TST-02 form (Task 15) with M19, M28 and Plan 2's sites M05 (A seeded from TIP on join/restart), M06 (raw snapshot pushed on top of TIP) and M07 (no first-parent fast-forward check), which 11-testing §3.4 lists in the INV-NOLOSS row and TC-ENG-02/TC-ENG-13 cite for M05; M01–M04 wait for Plan 7's mutation gate because their sites would trip L-GIT-FORBIDDEN; M37 (a CS published before its boundary) and M47 (a failing edit check discards or blocks the last passing capture) are deferred to Plan 5 with their oracle O-EDITCHECK (R3-49) and the hook-fed checkpoint trigger, whose site is Plan 5's (no Plan 3 code captures on a timer or at PostToolUse); Plan 3's tests that cite "the M47 shape" (Task 10) pin the behaviour M47 breaks with no mutant site — 11-testing §3.9 rows, TC-TST-02 — cost if wrong: a mutant site sits in `dist/` until Plan 7 strips it (as R-29); Plan 5 adds the M37/M47 sites to Plan 3's `captureStep`/`settleCandidate`. (data OQ-16, T-09; baseline Q13)
- **R3-51 CI budgets and the exit artifact.** Ruling: each PR leg runs `npm run sim:seeds -- --profile edits --seeds 70`, `--profile faults --seeds 15` and `--profile holds --seeds 15` (the X-113 rule: 100 per leg, each merged non-`edits` profile at its final count, `edits` the remainder; amended by the reconciliation from 60/20/20) with Plan 2's per-leg offsets, 4 child processes, job timeout 45 min, leg times reported against 11-testing §3.11's 20-minute budget; `sim-exit.yml` adds 1,000 `holds` seeds per OS in shards sized by R3-55 and R3-58 (10 of 100 when the measured seed time allows) and runs `npm run test:sim` once per OS; the roadmap row 3 artifact is one manually dispatched `sim-exit.yml` run on the plan's final commit with all three OS jobs green; the nightly 10,000/2,000/1,000 budgets and ≥ 100 kills per crashpoint stay Plan 7's — glossary §20 CI row, R-33 — cost if wrong: a leg over 20 minutes is reported, not failed. (data OQ-23, X-06, X-07)
- **R3-52 Crash matrix.** Ruling: no new crashpoint name; Task 16 kills every one of the 14 names (the names Plan 2 places, read from the catalogue `test/crashpoints.json`, X-116; with `registry.cas` at both call sites) once in each of 3 `holds` seeds, half followed by the author of a just-applied row deleting it and `git gc --prune=now`, and asserts O-STATE, O-REACH (every oid a record names exists), INV-GROUP and INV-NOLOSS after restart; PARK, RESTORE, the markers park, the collision take and the repair reach those names through `writeLocal`'s journaled intent — TST-06's SIM form, 05 F7 — cost if wrong: Plan 7's nightly finds what 3 seeds miss. (data T-07)

**Added by the plan review (2026-10-07):**
- **R3-53 Non-portable names no record can carry.** Ruling: a NOT_ADMITTED `portability` path that is not `stageable()` (it fails SEC-35 `validPathSyntax` — `a:b.txt`, a backslash, an NFD spelling — or carries U+FFFD or a dotgit-alias component, P2-DOTGIT) gets no record: glossary §9 requires record paths to be NFC and valid, `record.ts` refuses them, the fold refuses them in any `hold` op (PRO-21) and the snapshot never reads them (P2-CARRY), so no `h1` can exist. Instead `portabilityStep` raises `PATH_NOT_PORTABLE {path, rule}` (no `id`; `rule` from `portable(p, others)`) to status and the log once per (path, episode) and returns the path in `unrecorded`, and never puts it into any CS (`cs.observe` is not called: a CS's member paths reach `encodeCsPaths`, the CS table and the cache, which accept only valid paths); Task 10 makes `portabilityStep` hand that set to `ChangeSets.blockPaths` at every tick (in memory, recomputed from each tick's `notAdmitted`), and while `blocked()` is non-empty every group holding a human or recovered CS (open, or ready and unpublished) is not ready — the bad name is a human creation (Claude's Write is denied by G1 `PORTABILITY`, Plan 5), so the human-side change-set is the one DAT-32 withholds — a readiness gate, not a PIN: no record, no bundle, no dependency PIN, no `held/` entry; `publishPending` and the governor's pending input (`allowedPending`) follow the same groups, and a rename to a portable name or a deletion empties the set at the next tick (the deletion of `src/old.py` and the new name then publish in one commit); every path of a group the gate keeps back is listed in `not_converged` as `blocked_publish` and the blocked path itself too (Task 12a), so INV-CONVERGE holds while the name waits; start and restart run their first detection tick and `portabilityStep` before their first publish, so no start-time capture publishes the human CS around the gate (Task 12b) — DAT-32 ("its change-set MUST be withheld whole … never publishes the deletion alone"); DAT-33 and I-DAT-09 ("a record for every non-portable new path, `a:b.txt` included") conflict with glossary §9, SEC-35 and PRO-21 for these names, and a deletion published alone would break INV-NOSILENT — cost if wrong: such a CS waits with no record to `--discard`; the human renames or deletes the file, as `PATH_NOT_PORTABLE`'s text says. (review F01; data D-10)
- **R3-54 A Claude restore outside HLD-59's states.** Ruling: `claudeRestoreRefusal` returns a reason, not a message code — `'not_owner' | 'binary' | 'quarantine' | 'state'`, or null when HLD-59 admits the restore — and `resolve()` with a seat actor returns `{kind: 'claude_refused', reason}` for a non-null reason; Plan 3 writes none of them to status or the log as a code; Plan 5, which binds `cowork_resolve` (R3-02), maps `binary` → `RESTORE_BINARY_HUMAN_ONLY`, `quarantine` → `QUARANTINE_HUMAN_ONLY`, `not_owner` → 06's `NOT_OWN_RECORD`, and `state` to the code 06-claude §3.16.3 or the controller assigns (neither 05 §1 nor 06 §3.16.3 has one: a recorded gap; *closed by X-14: Plan 5 R5-41's mapping — `not_owner` → `NOT_OWN_RECORD`, `binary` → `RESTORE_BINARY_HUMAN_ONLY`, `quarantine` → `QUARANTINE_HUMAN_ONLY`, `state` → `TERMINAL_RECORD` when the record is terminal else `RESTORE_NOT_ALLOWED`, and a `locked` result → `PATH_BUSY`; Plan 3's reasons are unchanged*) — 05 §2's code table marks `HOLD_GUARDS_REMAIN` "CLI refusal only (verbs Claude cannot run, SEC-27)" and 06 §3.16.3 owns the MCP refusal codes — cost if wrong: Plan 5 renames one reason. (review F10)
- **R3-55 SIM test time.** Ruling: the wall time per `holds` seed on each OS (p50 and p95 over the PR-leg `holds` seeds — 15 per leg by the X-113 rule, 20 in the original draft) is recorded in the ledger and the exit shards are sized from it (who measures when: R3-58) — seeds per shard ≤ 0.8 × 120 min × 4 (parallel) ÷ p95 seed time, else more shards (the 1,000 seeds per OS unchanged); every SIM integration test that runs several seeds or a long sweep (Task 14's `holds-o-*` and the 1,000-trial `mask.fuzz`, Task 15's `holds-p7-*`, `holds-pdat-*`, `edits-n-0`/`faults-n-0` and `holds-n-0` with forced full scans, Task 16's `holds-x-*`, `edits-x-*`, `faults-x-*` and the crash-matrix seeds) runs its first seed (the fuzz: 100 trials) under `npm test` and all of them under `npm run test:sim` (environment `TEST_SIM_FULL=1`), which `sim-exit.yml` runs once per OS before its shards; every test file carries a `node:test` timeout of 10 min, and each PR leg reports `npm test`'s minutes next to its SIM minutes — the PR legs' 45-minute job timeout must hold `npm test` plus 100 SIM seeds, and an exit shard that times out would hide the oracle verdict the roadmap artifact needs — cost if wrong: a multi-seed regression is found by the exit run instead of the PR leg. (review F43)

**Added by the preflight (2026-10-08):**
- **R3-56 Empty held sides.** Ruling: `mirrorSync` puts `held/<id>/` only when `heldBlobs(r)` is non-empty (the pathless `host-reject` PIN's creation and terminal ops carry no `held.put`; the fold needs none for `paths: []`), and Task 6 creates no `unreadable`/`unstable` PIN for a path whose `h0 = A[p]` and `h1 = privateBlob(p)` are both ABSENT (a new file never staged): there is nothing to hold or publish (`S_pub` keeps `A[p]` = ABSENT), the path stays a publish-side transient exclusion and `holdStatusInputs` lists it in `not_converged` while it is excluded — `buildHeldTree` refuses an empty list, and PRO-21 requires `held/<id>/` for a non-terminal, non-`local_only` record with paths, so such a PIN could never fold valid and would end `HOLD_MIRROR_REJECTED`; exempting an empty held side in the fold would change board semantics (checkpoint bytes) for a record that holds nothing — cost if wrong: an unreadable never-staged new file has no record and no board op, visible only in this peer's status. (preflight table I1)
- **R3-57 Scope of a MASK assertion failure.** Ruling: an `assertSPub` failure (`maskStep`) sets `EngineCore.maskInvalid`, which stays set until the next passing `assertSPub` (S_pub stays invalid until recomputed), and while it is set `publishStep` sends no code commit; an `assertP` failure blocks only the code part of that `publishStep` (`PublishReport.maskInvalid = true`) and never sets `EngineCore.maskInvalid`, so P is composed and checked again in the next window with no new edit; both raise `HOLD_MASK_INVALID` and keep the allowed board-only ops flowing — R3-20's "this loop's publish" for a P built per window, and 05 §3.4's S_pub persisting between materializations for the other; under the earlier core.ts reading a peer whose ready CS is captured and which has no apply due never materializes again, so one `assertP` failure blocked its code publishing indefinitely — cost if wrong: a deterministic `assertP` failure repeats `HOLD_MASK_INVALID` once per window (logged, never pushed). (preflight table I2)
- **R3-58 Who measures the seed time.** Ruling: Task 13 records only the local wall time of `holds-smoke-0` and of a `TEST_SIM_FULL=1` 20-seed `holdsSetup` loop on the implementer's OS; Task 16 first lands the `ci.yml` holds legs (15 seeds per leg by the X-113 rule; 20 in the original draft) and `sim-exit.yml` with a provisional s = 20, records each OS's p50/p95 per-seed time from the first green PR run in the ledger, and then sets s = min(100, ⌊0.8 × 120 min × 4 ÷ p95⌋) in the same task — the PR legs run `holds` seeds only once Task 16 adds `run-seeds --profile holds` and the legs, so Task 13 cannot measure them — cost if wrong: one extra Task 16 commit resizing the shards. (preflight table I4; coherence "R3-55's per-OS seed timing")
- **R3-59 Provenance: advance sites and load points.** Ruling: (1) `ProvenanceJournal.advance` runs through one helper `advanceProvenance(core, oldLaC)` (core.ts): `EngineCore.commit` calls it right after a CAS that moves `laC` (push ok, restart's decision commit, a join's first-apply commit) unless the caller passes the commit option `{provenance: 'caller'}`; `applyStep` passes that option and calls `advanceProvenance` itself right after `crashpoint('apply.state')` and `setCurrentA`; `lostStateRebuild`, which writes the state without `commit()`, calls it after `writeLostState` and `attachState` — so 11-testing §3.6's `apply.state` anchor ("before … the provenance journal [is] updated", the only anchor naming the journal) stays true and a kill there leaves the journal one commit behind, which `load` advances, while `publish.ts` (Task 9's in Wave E) needs no edit; (2) the join point is the live code of the oldest state-reflog entry's `laB` (`L0` at genesis, TIP at a join), not its `laC` (a joiner's first state has `laC = L_b`, ENG-52); (3) the journal keeps its load point, `advance` appends only commits after the later of `old` and that point, and it is a no-op before `load()`; (4) genesis and join call `prov.load(tip, tip)` (an empty journal; `tip` from `liveCodeOf`) right after `attachState` and before their first apply, and a restart calls `prov.load(view.laC, await provRebuildFrom(core))` right after `attachState(core, v)` and before `resolveWriteIntent`; Task 8 adds these calls to `start.ts`, `restart.ts` and the test bootstrap (Task 12b only tests them) — Plan 2's join creates the state with `laC = L_b` and moves it to TIP through an ordinary `EngineCore.commit` at its first apply, so a commit-time append before any load would turn the team's whole pre-join history into 14-day revert protection, and the Global Constraints say Plan 3 moves no anchored step — cost if wrong: a laC-moving site this list misses appends nothing until the next restart's `load` catches up (one commit behind is advanced, never rebuilt). (p2 "Provenance at join and restart", "Provenance append moved before the apply.state anchor"; coherence "Nothing loads the provenance journal")
- **R3-60 Park and discard writes survive a restart.** Ruling: after `WRITTEN` and before the registry's final commit (so an interrupted operator's `inflight` entry is still set and `settleHoldOps` completes both), the park family (`park`, `markersPark`, S6, `parkAllForStop`) and `--discard` (including `discardHolds`) commit one state transition whose only change is `snap := editTreeIndexOnly(snap, p → written entry)` for the written paths (Plan 2's `Transition.snap`; `A`, the journal and `keep` unchanged — an omitted `keep` keeps `view.keep`); restart's unconditional `core.snapshot.reseed(view.snap)` (Plan 2 Task 15) then reproduces the `Snapshotter.setEntry` entry, so those paths are no unobserved delta after a restart; restart's reseed itself is unchanged — Plan 2's restart reseeds the private index from `state:snap` on every restart (R-01's re-forming of the open human CS), so `setEntry` alone does not survive it and 11 parked paths would trip FREEZE(MASS) after every stop; the snap tree is written at a hold-record occasion (glossary §26.1 W6 (c)) — cost if wrong: one extra state commit per park or discard. (p2 "Snapshotter.setEntry does not survive Plan 2's restart")
- **R3-61 The degraded status rung for hold conditions.** Ruling: Plan 2's state precedence and rungs stay (01-engine §3.16: `degraded` whenever `pending.not_converged` is non-empty, `message_code` of the highest condition); when the state is `degraded` and no specific Plan 2 degraded condition outranks the generic not-converged one, `message_code` = `lastEvent` (fed by `core.holdCode`) when set, else `HOLD_DIVERGED_REGIONS {n, age}` when `diverged.n > 0`, else Plan 2's degraded code; the `ok` ladder becomes `BRANCH_REWRITTEN`, else `HOST_THROTTLED`, else `lastEvent`, else `CS_NO_CHECKS_CONFIGURED`, else null — R3-46 lists every ON_DISK record path and every R3-53 path in `not_converged`, so `HOLD_DIVERGED_REGIONS` and `PATH_NOT_PORTABLE` could never be the message of the `ok` state — cost if wrong: one rung's message order. (p2 "HOLD_DIVERGED_REGIONS and PATH_NOT_PORTABLE are placed in the 'ok' ladder")
- **R3-62 No re-MASK in recordStep.** Ruling: `recordStep` does not recompute `S_pub`; it passes `maskStep`'s `conflicted` ids to `evaluateRecords`, and `S_pub`, `core.ranges` and the `mask` trace event change only at the next `maskStep` (one `mask` event per materialization) — 01-engine §3.5 step 4 computes `S_pub := MASK(S_disk)` once per materialization and uses it "for detection and status only", P is composed from `MASK(R)` at publish time with the current A, and a second computation would need its own `assertSPub` and a second event per materialization for `oracleMask` — cost if wrong: `heldRanges` and status reflect the pre-apply MASK until the next materializing iteration. (table "whether recordStep's re-MASK updates sPub"; coherence "recordStep's 're-MASK' is never defined")
- **R3-63 Composability in the oracles; compose events around T0.** Ruling: (1) a `compose` event's `pairs` are every journaled ready pair at that composition and `groups[].ready` is the engine's readiness; Task 14's oracles call a group composable only when the event marks it `ready`, it holds no non-terminal ON_DISK PIN with guards in `records(registry)` and every csId of the group is among the pairs (both test-side), so a group that the R3-53 gate or a missing passing capture keeps back is not composable and must keep `A` in P, while an engine that marks a test-side-held group ready still fails; (2) a T0 trial composition of a group whose only held PIN carries only `t0` emits no `compose` event; when T0 passes, the release is a registry commit and P is recomposed, which emits the `compose` event that precedes Bnew, so the landed commit's event never shows a group under a `t0` PIN; every other recomposition (after a K7 PIN, a drop, a dependency PIN, a CAS re-merge) emits its event as before — a purely test-side readiness rule misjudges record-less gates (R3-53) and the t0 trial — cost if wrong: an engine bug marking a composable group not ready only delays it (INV-CONVERGE catches a group that never publishes). (table I8; coherence "Task 14's composable group ignores R3-53")
- **R3-64 The INV-NOLOSS resurrection clause.** Ruling: the clause Task 15 proposed is adopted into R3-48 — a marker in `MarkerModel.retired()` (an informed removal by a peer whose A or disk held it, with no noted re-add since) that appears in `live_code(board tip)` fails INV-NOLOSS — and Task 15's M19 and "stays quiet on legitimate re-adds" tests are unconditional — 11-testing §3.4 lists M19 in the INV-NOLOSS row, a static MASK can only resurrect a teammate's removal (the record keeps its `h1`), and TST-08's reference model already makes an informed removal authoritative — cost if wrong: a re-add the model fails to note fires INV-NOLOSS falsely (Task 15's quiet-on-re-adds test pins the noted forms). (table I11; coherence "M19's pairing with INV-NOLOSS")
- **R3-65 Codes reserved for later plans.** Ruling: `HOLD_PIN_CONTEXT` and `HOLD_NOT_SHARED` (carried only by a seat's next hook, HLD-22, HLD-23), `HOLD_RETURNED` (HLD-09 offer events Plan 3 never fires, R3-06) and `QUARANTINE_HUMAN_ONLY` (Plan 5's MCP mapping, R3-54; Plan 7's review) stay in `HOLD_CODES` as declared codes with no Plan 3 raise site, and R3-08 no longer says Plan 3 writes the first two to status and log; `RESTORE_BINARY_HUMAN_ONLY` and `QUARANTINE_HUMAN_ONLY` leave `ResolveRefusal` (`resolve()` returns `claude_refused` instead, R3-54) and keep their `NEXT_COMMAND` entries — Plan 3 has no seat, so a status line for a seat-hook code would tell the human nothing `HOLD_CREATED` does not — cost if wrong: Plan 5 adds two raise sites. (table "Four codes in HOLD_CODES have no raise site"; coherence "Three codes are in HOLD_CODES with no task that raises them")
- **R3-66 Early start of Task 1.** Ruling: Task 1 may start before Plan 2's Tasks 15–20 merge only once Plan 2's Task 15 round 2 and Task 16 round 1 (which edit `core.ts`, `apply.ts`, `publish.ts`, `diskEq.ts`, `changesets.ts` and `treeEdit.ts`) have merged into feat/p2-engine and feat/p3-holds is rebased onto it; Task 3 starts after Task 1 (it depends on it); Task 1's re-baseline covers only the suites of Plan 2 Tasks 1–14, and Task 2's re-baseline adds Plan 2 Task 17's `eol-bom` suite (`loop.ts`) — starting Task 1 earlier guarantees merge conflicts and a second review of Task 1 — cost if wrong: Task 1 waits for two fix rounds. (p2 "R3-01's early start overlaps Plan 2 fix rounds"; table "Task 1's re-baseline scope")
- **R3-67 A failed collision-take capture ends GONE.** Ruling: the guard-less OFF_DISK PARKED capture of a collision take whose take fails (Task 5) or whose TIP write did not happen by restart (`settleHoldOps`) ends GONE (its bytes are still on disk), and `oracleOffDisk` exempts it beside the marker companion — test-side: a guard-less OFF_DISK PARKED PATCH with `h0` ABSENT whose path had a non-terminal `collision` record when it was created — RESOLVED_DISCARD would record a human discard (mirrored, a `discard` trace, INV-NOLOSS's discarded set) of bytes nobody discarded, and R3-15's failed-companion rule has the same shape — cost if wrong: one more exemption in O-OFFDISK. (table I7)
- **R3-68 Capture order before the first table.** Ruling: while no CS table is journaled (Plan 2's state; `restore` given a null journaled table, R3-69), `ChangeSets.capture` assigns `order = n` and forms no multi-CS group, exactly as Plan 2, and `StateStore.commit` keeps Plan 2's `order === n` check; once a table is journaled (written by `captureStep`, and by `captureReady`/the push-ok update only when one exists), every pair's order comes from it and the table rule applies; the first table's `next_order` is `max(max n over journaled pairs, meta.cs_human_next − 1) + 1` — otherwise a Plan 2 loop capture between Task 9 and Task 12a would journal `<peer>/human/5` with order 1 after a restart with `cs_human_next = 5` and no pair, which Plan 2's commit refuses — cost if wrong: none (the two modes are separated by whether a table is journaled). (table I9)
- **R3-69 Restoring the change-set table.** Ruling: `ChangeSets.restore(ready, nextN, journaled?, cache?)`: `journaled` undefined keeps the current in-memory table (the error-path restores in Plan 2's `apply.ts` and `publish.ts`; at construction there is none: Plan 2 behaviour); `journaled` null = no table journaled yet (R3-68); a non-null `journaled` (`readCsTable(repo, view)`) always supplies `orders`, `next_order`, `passing`, the publish counts, closed CSs and `seats`; `cache` (`readCsCache(core)`) supplies only open-CS membership and only when it names the current state commit — a null or stale cache turns every unpublished delta outside a ready pair into one `recovered` CS (HLD-36) and changes nothing the journaled table supplies; `attachState` stays synchronous and a new `async attachCsTable(core, view)` (core.ts) reads both and calls `restore`, awaited right after every `attachState` call — Task 12a adds it in `core.ts`, `start.ts`, `restart.ts`, `recover.ts` and `test/helpers/coreFixture.ts` (12a runs before 12b, which edits `start.ts`/`restart.ts` again) — the journaled table is the truth (R3-40, HLD-36), so a cache lost by a crash between a state commit and `writeCsCache` must not make non-passing pairs ready or restart `next_order` below existing orders, and an async `attachState` would leave floating promises at its synchronous call sites — cost if wrong: one more await per state attach. (table I10; p2 "CS-table restore is async")
- **R3-70 Two small choices.** Ruling: (1) `escalationStep` raises, for each record created since its last call, `HOLD_CREATED` (or `HOLD_CONFLICTED`) and then, for a binary-path `revert` PIN, `HOLD_BINARY_WHOLE_FILE {hid, path}` again, so status ends on the binary code; `revertStep` still writes `HOLD_BINARY_WHOLE_FILE` to the log once per record and sets `holdCode` (Task 8's test checks only that); (2) own `hold` op rejections with reason `generic` or `type` are deterministic non-race rejections recorded like `validator` (`MirrorRejectReason` gains both; `HOLD_MIRROR_REJECTED` once) — a code raised at creation would need a hook in every creator while the ladder already runs every loop, and a `generic`/`type` own rejection repeats identically on every re-draft — cost if wrong: the order of two status lines; a record left non-durable by a rejection a re-draft would have fixed. (table "HOLD_CREATED and HOLD_BINARY_WHOLE_FILE order", "Own hold rejections with 'generic' or 'type'")

## Cross-plan assumptions (resolved 2026-10-08)

Plan 3 was drafted before Plans 4–8b and had no cross-plan section; the reconciliation settles every point where a later plan reads or edits a Plan 3 name (the "Ruling" column cites `RECONCILIATION.md` §1, not the data map's `X-nn`). Plan 3 imports nothing from Plans 4–8b: each later plan binds a Plan 3 port or calls a Plan 3 export, in its own file (X-110 import order: `… src/board → src/since → src/holds → src/claims → src/engine → src/claude → src/coord …`; X-111 wiring: `EngineDeps` = Plan 2 base ← `plan3Ports()` ← `plan4Ports(host)` ← later plans, each plan editing only its own line).

| Consumer | What it takes from Plan 3 (resolved form) | Ruling |
|---|---|---|
| Plan 2 / F04-a | F04-a (`src/since/report.ts`, the Plan 2 follow-up F-P2-1) owns the `sinceReport` core; Plan 3 Task 19 adds the hold-transition rows (PARKED, `offer=returned`, SUBSUMED) and their fixtures after F04-a merges. The `foldCommit` loop that Plan 3 Task 3 and 8a Task 2 both changed follows the R-V02 merge order, and Plan 2 code constructs no `FoldStepResult` literal. | X-12, X-76, X-90 |
| Plan 4 | Binds `EditCheckPort`, `T0RegionsPort`, `PublishGatePort`, `SymbolPort` through `plan4Ports(host)` (the only binder of the four); narrows `T0Verdict.findings` to `T0Finding` and `EditCheckVerdict.entries` to `EditCheckEntry` (both stay assignable to Plan 3's element types); passes its `CoreOptions.ports` as the function form reading `deps.regions.host`; edits `ports.ts`, `csStep.ts`, `publish.ts`, `loop.ts` only after Plan 3 merges; writes no `last_check` itself (its verdict reaches `settleCandidate`); its revert prediction (Algorithm REV step 6, `Engine.predictRevert`, called by Plan 7's revert verb) takes Task 17's `revertInverse` results. `oracleOffDisk`'s exception is accepted (Plan 3 reviewer); Plan 3's own-hold-line fold test is unaffected by Plan 4's fixture rewrites. | X-07, X-08, X-15, X-32, X-33, X-09 |
