**Spec:** 11-testing §3.3 run steps 4–5, §3.6 (the 14 names; `registry.cas` two call sites), §3.11 (PR legs, 20-minute leg budget), TST-02, TST-03, TST-06, TST-07, TST-09 (artifacts; the shrinker stays Plan 7), TST-13; glossary §20 CI row ("200 seeds/OS per PR"), §21 INV-CONVERGE (K = 20 + 40); 01-engine ENG-59; 05 §7 F7, INV-HLD-DURABLE; 11-testing §7 TC-ENG-12 (SIM form); roadmap row 3 exit; rulings R-31, R-32, R-33 (carried), R3-49, R3-51, R3-52.

**Notes:**
- **CI per PR (R3-51, the cross-plan rule X-113):** every `ci.yml` leg (windows-latest, macos-latest, ubuntu-latest × Node 20 and Node 24) runs, after `npm test`, `npm run sim:seeds -- --profile edits --seeds 70`, `-- --profile faults --seeds 15` and `-- --profile holds --seeds 15` (100 per leg; each merged non-`edits` profile at its final count — `faults` 15, `holds` 15 — and `edits` the remainder; later plans' profiles take their counts from `edits`), each with `--from 0` on Node 20 legs and `--from 100` on Node 24 legs (Plan 2's per-leg offsets) and `--parallel 4`, job `timeout-minutes: 45`; a last step sums the three reports' `ms` and writes `SIM <m> min of the 20-minute leg budget (11-testing §3.11)` and `npm test <n> min` to the job summary, as a `::warning::` annotation when the SIM part is over 20 minutes (reported, never failed); `npm test` runs each multi-seed SIM integration test on its first seed only (R3-55). That is 100 seeds per leg, 200 per OS per PR (glossary §20 CI row, TST-13); the nightly 10,000/2,000/1,000 budgets and ≥ 100 kills per crashpoint stay Plan 7's.
- **Exit run (R3-51, R3-55, roadmap row 3):** `sim-exit.yml` (workflow_dispatch and its Plan 2 schedule) runs `npm run test:sim` once per OS, keeps its 10 `edits` shards per OS and adds `holds` shards per OS: `run-seeds --profile holds --seeds <s> --from <s·k> --prefix exit- --parallel 4` for k = 0…⌈1,000 / s⌉ − 1, `timeout-minutes: 120`, each uploading its report JSON, where s = min(100, ⌊0.8 × 120 min × 4 ÷ the p95 per-seed time of that OS⌋) (10 shards of 100 when the measured time allows); the p95 is measured here (R3-58): this task first lands the `ci.yml` holds legs and `sim-exit.yml` with a provisional s = 20, records each OS's p50/p95 per-seed time from the first green PR run in the ledger, then sets s in a following commit of this task; with every profile asserting `exitOracles(ns, {holds: {markers}})`, this checks INV-NOLOSS, INV-MASK and INV-GROUP on 1,000 `holds` seeds per OS and Plan 2's six exit oracles on 1,000 `edits` seeds per OS. `npm run sim:exit:holds` = `tsx tools/sim/run-seeds.ts --profile holds --seeds 1000 --parallel 8` is the local rehearsal, never the artifact.
- **Crash matrix (R3-52, TST-06 SIM form):** one run per seed (`holds-crash-0`, `-1`, `-2`; `npm test` runs `holds-crash-0`, `npm run test:sim` all three, R3-55), driven by `test/sim/crashMatrix.ts` (Plan 2 Task 19's matrix driver, extracted) with `holdsSetup` as its setup: the 14 names of `test/crashpoints.json` that Plan 2 places (read from the catalogue, not a hard-coded list: X-116 makes it the single catalogue, which later plans append to) (the 13 of ENG-59 plus `registry.cas`) are armed one after another on the peer and step the driver draws, the next armed only after the previous kill's restart; `registry.cas` is armed twice, once at an odd hit (before the CAS) and once at an even hit of the same registry commit (after the CAS); names the seed's generated ops do not reach use the driver's Plan 2 lead-ins (a board-rewind incident and `Engine.recover` for `recover.displaced`/`recover.reanchor`, a two-CS teammate publish for `apply.between_groups`). After every second kill (k even) the author of one row the killed peer's last `apply` event wrote — the peer whose publish that apply wrote, found from the row's marker; rows authored by the team writer (`nonconforming_push`) or by the killed peer itself are skipped, and the kill counts toward the half only when such a row exists — deletes that row by an informed `edit_own`-style write (its own line, so the deletion is untainted, HLD-27), the test asserts the deletion lands in `live_code`, and `git gc --prune=now` runs in every clone and the remote. The run's oracles are `exitOracles(ns, {holds: {markers}})` plus `oracleState()` and `oracleReach()`.

**Tests:**
- `"exitOracles(ns) and exitOracles(ns, {}) return exactly Plan 2's six in order; exitOracles(ns, {holds: {markers}}) returns those six followed by oracleMask, oracleGroup, oracleReady, oracleAnd, oracleOffDisk, oracleLiveRecord, oracleAtomicPub, oracleNoSilent, oracleNoloss and oracleHeldSecret"` — R3-49, TST-07.
- `"every exit oracle passes on holds seeds holds-x-0…holds-x-3, edits seeds edits-x-0…edits-x-3 and faults seeds faults-x-0, faults-x-1, each run through run-shard with exitOracles(ns, {holds: {markers}})"` — `npm test` runs `holds-x-0`, `edits-x-0` and `faults-x-0`, `npm run test:sim` all ten (R3-55); TST-07 (every SIM run asserts every oracle), CS-52, X-07.
- `"every Task 14 oracle stays green on Task 15's NOLOSS plant while INV-NOLOSS fails"` — the TC-TST-02 NOLOSS plant (one unpublished marker row deleted from its author's file without telling the model) run with `exitOracles(ns, {holds: {markers}})`: only `oracleNoloss` fires (Task 15 runs in parallel with Task 14, so this cross-check lives here; TC-TST-02, TST-07).
- `"the 14-name crash matrix on holds-crash-0, -1 and -2: each Plan 2 name killed once per seed and registry.cas twice (before and after the CAS); after each restart oracleState, oracleReach, INV-GROUP and INV-NOLOSS hold, the row its author deleted after an even kill lands in live_code and never reappears in any later live_code, and each run reaches quiescence passing every exit oracle"` — TST-06 (SIM form), T-07, R3-52, TC-ENG-12 (SIM form).
- `"crashMatrix.ts drives Plan 2's sim.crash runs unchanged"` — `test/integration/sim.crash.test.ts` imports `runCrashMatrix` and its faults-profile assertions pass as before the extraction (Plan 2 Task 19).
- `"05 F7 (SIM): a KilledAt at registry.cas before the CAS leaves the registry ref at the old commit and after the CAS at the new commit (whose parent is the old); the restarted record set equals the old set plus exactly the ids and revs of the killed commit, with no id twice and none missing"` — 05 F7, HLD-01.
- `"Review Focus 3: registry.cas killed after the CAS inside a resolve --park, an S6 markers park, a resolve --restore, a resolve --discard of an ON_DISK record, a collision take (resolve --publish on a collision) and an apply-side K4 displacement, and apply.group.phase1 / apply.group.phase2 killed inside the same operators' writeLocal groups, each followed by git gc --prune=now: the operator's bytes are in a registry record before the disk changed, restart completes or undoes it by observation (settleMarkersParks, the writer's intent rollback) before the first publish, the registry is the old or the new commit, every record blob exists, and INV-NOLOSS holds at every step"` — Review Focus 3, R3-15, R3-23, R3-52.
- `"oracleState fails on a planted update-ref that moves a peer's state ref to a commit whose LA_C is not on the previous LA_C's first-parent chain, and on a reflog entry whose old value is not the previous new value"` — O-STATE.
- `"oracleReach fails on a planted registry commit naming a blob that gc --prune=now removed, and passes after gc_prune ops on holds-crash-0"` — O-REACH, F-FS-4.
- `"quiescence with Plan 3 timers: a holds script that creates a revert record early, then {advance: 540_000}, then (step 55) leaves an INCOMING lock deferral, reaches T* within 400 rounds although the 60 s INCOMING stub and the record's +10 min HOLD_REVERT_HELD event push during quiescence (each restarting K, the event's 10-minute mark falling inside the K window), and then makes zero push attempts in the 40 extra rounds"` — and on every holds seed `holdTimersSettled` (Task 13) keeps T* back until no hold timer is due within the next 40 rounds, so a timer's push never lands in the extra rounds; X-07 (§3.3 run step 5 with hold timers), INV-CONVERGE, INV-HLD-BUDGET consistency, R-32.
- `"run-seeds --profile holds runs holdsSetup with 60 steps unless --steps, crash rate 0.02, and exitOracles(ns, {holds: {markers}}); --profile edits and faults now also pass a MarkerModel and SimOptions.settled = holdTimersSettled; each report line carries profile, steps, kills and per-kind counts"` — an `edits` seed whose informed edit of a teammate's line makes a `revert` record shortly before quiescence declares T* only after its +10 min `HOLD_REVERT_HELD` event has folded, and the 40 extra rounds make zero push attempts (X-06, R3-49, R3-37, R-32).
- `"run-seeds exits 0 when 3 holds seeds pass, 1 when a holds seed fails under --mutant M38, 2 on --profile holds2; a failing seed prints its seed tuple and rerun command and keeps <root>/artifacts with op-log.jsonl, remote.bundle and p0-local.bundle, p1-local.bundle, p2-local.bundle holding refs/cowork-local/*"` — X-07 (TST-09 artifacts), X-06.
- `"ci.yml: each of the six legs runs sim:seeds --profile edits --seeds 70, --profile faults --seeds 15 and --profile holds --seeds 15 with --from 0 on Node 20 legs and --from 100 on Node 24 legs, --parallel 4, timeout-minutes 45, and a final step that reports the leg's SIM minutes against 20 as a warning, never a failure; the seeds per OS per PR total 200"` — R3-51, TST-13, glossary §20 CI row, X-06; rev 2.5 (X-113): `ci-config.test.ts` asserts the rule, not these numbers — every leg sums to 100, every merged non-`edits` profile has its fixed count from one table (`faults` 15, `holds` 15, `claims` 15, `seats` 15, `coord` 15, `lifecycle` 10, the last four for later plans), and `edits` has the remainder, so a later plan adding its profile in any merge order only edits the workflow and the table.
- `"sim-exit.yml keeps workflow_dispatch and its schedule and, per OS, runs npm run test:sim once, 10 edits shards and holds shards covering seeds 0…999 exactly once (run-seeds --profile holds --seeds s --from s·k --prefix exit- --parallel 4, s ≤ 100 sized from the p95 per-seed time this task recorded per OS from the PR legs, R3-58, timeout-minutes 120, report uploaded); the ledger names each OS's p50/p95 and the s derived from it; package.json scripts sim:exit:holds (run-seeds --profile holds --seeds 1000 --parallel 8) and test:sim (npm test with TEST_SIM_FULL=1)"` — R3-51, R3-55, roadmap row 3 exit, CS-52 (exit run).
- `"npm run ci still ends with sim:smoke and Plan 2's 5-seed edits run, and Plan 2's ci-config assertions are extended, not dropped"` — TST-13 (carried).

**Spec-edit batch (rev 2.5, X-04, X-03):** after owner approval, the plan's last merged task (this one, unless Task 19 or follow-up 3-V merges later, which then carries it) applies Plan 3's spec batch — the "Spec conflicts and gaps" list in the Scope rulings as the reconciliation trims it, plus the ledger's spec edits (02 §3.1 R3-74, PRO-21 terminal holds, R3-75) — against rev 2.5, after Plan 8a's batch and before Plan 4's, naming the batch it follows; it re-edits no D1/D2/D4/D5 text that rev 2.5 already carries.

**Exit evidence (recorded in the ledger, not a unit test):** the roadmap row 3 artifact is one manually dispatched `sim-exit.yml` run on the plan's final commit whose three OS jobs (windows-latest, macos-latest, ubuntu-latest) are all green — `npm run test:sim` and 1,000 `holds` seeds per OS (shards sized by R3-55) passing INV-NOLOSS, INV-MASK, INV-GROUP and every other exit oracle, and 1,000 `edits` seeds per OS still passing Plan 2's six; the ledger records the run URL, the commit, per-OS seed and failure counts, the PR legs' SIM minutes against the 20-minute budget, and the crash matrix's kill count per name. A local `npm run sim:exit:holds` is a rehearsal, not the artifact.

**Crashpoints:** none new (the matrix kills the existing 14 Plan 2 names; `test/crashpoints.json` unchanged, L-CRASHPOINTS green). **Lint:** none.

**Crashpoints:** none new. **Lint:** none new (L-HOLD-STORES names the `revertInverse()` exception from Task 8, F-P3-6).

**Notes:**
- The verb is Plan 7's (preview/confirm, SEC-25, `PREVIEW_STALE`, the daemon worker at background priority, `RF_REVERT_FROZEN` before computing, the `RF_REVERT_*`/`AMBIGUOUS_ID`/`RF_SHOW_UNKNOWN_ID` mapping of `LocateResult`, the test-only `takeTheirs` switch); the step-6 prediction is Plan 4's `Engine.predictRevert` over `revertInverse` results; the undo view's rendering and `cowork_show` are Plan 5's (and Plan 6's `CoordHost.undo`). This task builds what they call and nothing they own.
- Confirm order (Algorithm REV step 8, as Plan 7 calls it): `revertInverse(…, {mode: 'verb'})` again → `hash-object -w` of each result (§8 Q17 (b), the apply occasion) → one `writeLocalGroup` with origin `revert:<of>` (`of` = the reverted CS's id, known before the write) and the digested disk blobs as expected values → on `WRITTEN`, `openRevertCs(core, of, written)` (ObservedWrites, CS, mark, journal line, in that order). A crash between `WRITTEN` and `openRevertCs` leaves the written paths as an ordinary unobserved human delta with no mark and no "revert of" label: the lines it removes or re-adds are this peer's own (step 5 guaranteed it), so the revert detector stays quiet, and only the revert shrink exemption (decision D2) is lost (the ordinary guard applies, the documented loss of §8 Q18).
- `src/holds/revertCs.ts` imports nothing from `src/engine`; `busyPaths` is synchronous and memory-only, and the DISK_EQ test runs per path inside `revertInverse`. `RevertMarks` lives on `ChangeSets` so `rebase` moves it without an engine call; `changesets.ts` imports the class from `revertCs.ts` at runtime, and `revertCs.ts` imports only types from `changesets.ts` (no runtime cycle).
